# Roadmap

datawatch is a Go daemon that runs autonomous AI coding sessions, called
Automata (internally PRDs), across several LLM backends. The v10.0.0 release
is the point where the remaining backlog is built by datawatch's own Automata
engine instead of by hand. "Pre-v10.0.0" means the work that has to land first,
in the order below, so that engine can be trusted with the rest.

Shipped work is summarized in [HISTORY.md](HISTORY.md). Status below was
checked against the live PRD registry on 2026-10-17. Each row links to its
design doc where one exists.

## Rules

All operating rules live in [`AGENT.md`](../../AGENT.md) — this file holds
project-tracking data only. If an operating rule ends up duplicated here,
move it to AGENT.md and leave a cross-reference instead.

**Folder hygiene** (operator-directed 2026-05-05): `docs/plans/` only holds
dated plan docs (`YYYY-MM-DD-*.md`) ≤ 7 days old and release notes for the
current minor version line. Tidy is **link-aware** (`scripts/tidy-plans.sh`,
plan-side logic in `scripts/plans_links.py`): a plan stays in this folder
as long as anything still references it — an open row in this registry,
any file under `scripts/`, a root rules file
(AGENT.md, CLAUDE.md, any CONTEXT.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, README.md,
SECURITY.md), or any doc under `docs/` outside `docs/plans/`. Only plans
past the 7-day cutoff with no such references and a closed registry row
(done / shipped / completed / merged / cancelled / landed, not vetoed by a
negation) move to `historical-plans/`; older release notes group into
`historical-releasenotes/<minor-version>/`. On each move, every relative
and absolute link pointing at the plan — markdown targets and path
mentions, in this registry and elsewhere — is rewritten to the new
location.

`scripts/check-plans-links.sh` is the broken-link gate: it scans the same
reference surfaces and prints only the **count** of references into
`docs/plans/` whose target no longer resolves (0 and exit 0 when clean);
broken targets are never listed, and it takes `--quiet` to stay silent at
zero. Registry hygiene (`scripts/plans_registry_hygiene.py`, run on every
tidy) removes duplicate sections (first wins, later bodies folded in),
merges all-closed tables into one, dedupes rows, trims verbose closed
status cells, and with `--status-file PATH` (an Automaton list export,
read as-is) corrects stale draft/not-started rows. Output everywhere is
counts only — never a list of files.

`scripts/release-smoke.sh` runs `tidy-plans.sh --check` (counts only, exit 1
if anything would move) first and **aborts the release** if the folder
needs tidying — run `scripts/tidy-plans.sh` (no flags) to do the moves and
the registry hygiene, then commit before re-running smoke. `--dry-run`
reports counts without changing anything.
See [`docs/howto/plans-folder-tidy.md`](../howto/plans-folder-tidy.md) for the folder rules, the daily Automation and every setting.

**PRD authoring checklist — local-environment leaks:** a PRD's specs,
test fixtures and docs must carry no operator-machine identifiers
(private or tailnet addresses, absolute home paths, personal email, or
any token from the gitignored local patterns file) — use the
placeholders from the AGENT.md rule "No local-environment leaks in git"
and say the real value lives in the operator config. `make check-leaks`
(`scripts/check-no-local-leaks.sh`) must pass before a task is done.

## Active order

- [Closed rows archived for 2026-11](roadmap-archive-2026-11.md), rows 7 to 379

Items run in this order, one at a time against this tree, until the write lock
(BL428 Phase 2) ships. Until then, sequencing is enforced by hand, not by the
daemon.

**Federation-aware shared-resource coordination** (additive scope inside
BL428/BL431/BL405, not a separate item): detecting a shared compute node or
billing account across federated peers via keyed-hash fingerprint exchange,
operator confirmation before merging capacity pools, and live cross-daemon
lease/back-off. Lands as BL428 §3c (row 8) and §4e (rows 12-13) in
[concurrency-and-budget-design.md](2026-10-14-concurrency-and-budget-design.md),
BL431's open question 14, and BL405 Phase 7.

| 95 | BL514 Lanes sit idle while eligible work is queued: lane fill (auto-start on a freed slot, decompose-ahead, idle alert) must be automatic, not a manual tick step (2026-10-19). Verified infrastructure (2026-10-19): both GPU machines have qwen3.8-64k:27b and hold it resident (local at a 65536 context; remote at <remote-node-address>, about 18 GB VRAM). The remote box answers `datawatch llm test ollama-datawatch` and has done PRD work per the operator. The two GPU machines are ONE pool, per the operator (2026-10-19): which machine serves a given pi PRD is not a decision the supervisor makes, and it is not fixed per lane. Observed: a pi PRD session connected to the remote Ollama (<remote-node-address>:11434) at 17:29, and a pi session connected to the local Ollama (127.0.1.1:11434) at 17:49, so both machines do PRD work. Lane fill keeps the pool full using live connections (`ss -tnp | grep 11434`) and residency (`ollama ps`) as evidence, and must not call the pool idle without that check. Standing orders (operator, 2026-10-19): every queue holds at least two eligible items ready to start; an approved PRD whose project directory is the main checkout is recreated as a draft with no project directory (done for 2712d303, 88fdf815 and 01bcf5cb, now 79890001, 97fc0001 and b7890001), then decomposed and approved. Lane model (operator decision, 2026-10-19): two GPU channels (the pool) and two Claude channels. Claude usage is at 14% weekly with budget to spare, so when a Claude channel is free it runs decompose serially (one decompose thread at a time, never two), to clear backlog ahead of the workers; Claude channels still run no more than one PRD each. The registry lists only `qwen3.8:27b` on both ollama backends, not the 64k tag (registry gap, tracked here). DECISION 2026-10-20 (operator asked why merges waited): the datawatch project rules (`/home/<user>/workspace/datawatch/CLAUDE.md`, `/home/<user>/workspace/CLAUDE.md`, and supervisor rule 6) allow local commits and merges; only pushes need operator approval. The "do not commit" line in `/home/<user>/CLAUDE.md` belongs to another session's guardrail and was wrongly applied to supervisor merges. Supervisor merges go to local main, with renumbering, build and tests, and no push. LANE DECISION 2026-10-20 (operator): three Claude lanes (one PRD each, one decompose thread each) and two GPU channels; the lane count is enforced by the supervisor, not the daemon. Design: the owner releases its own output at the end of its work (the Automaton lifecycle removes its worktree and branch once safe, the session manager removes a session's temp files at session end), the periodic pass is only a safety net and reports how much it still has to do, and nothing is tied to this repository (any project, any default branch name). In place: `scripts/claude-burn.py`, cap and effort changes, rules in the supervisor rules document. | 358 | Shipped 2026-11-05 afternoon (9.97.4, patch), not yet deployed: rejudge reviews the current branch head instead of replaying a stale verdict (Automaton `da570001`). The rules-review verdict records the commit it was judged on; a rejudge replays the stored verdict only when the head and the judge inputs are unchanged and the judge verdict cache is on, otherwise a fresh judge runs on the current head. A rules-review hold (`needs_review` after retries were spent or with auto-fix off) accepts a rejudge once new commits are on the branch, and is refused before any judge runs when the head is unchanged. MCP reference and operations guide updated. No PWA change, so no datawatch-app issue | done | |

| 380 | Defects found 2026-11-06 11:00 (two Automata, one pattern): (1) a per-task worktree carries only the task's own verification script, not `.datawatch/verify/lib.sh` that every gate script sources, so the final gate task of `ee660001` failed with exit 127 ("lib.sh: No such file or directory") and was held after three attempts; repaired by copying the library in and retrying. Fix: restore the whole verify folder into a task worktree, or fail the task as an environment error and not as a worker failure. (2) After the rules review spends its one auto-fix retry an Automaton is `needs_review` for only about 15 minutes, then the stuck-running check flips it to `blocked`, and a blocked Automaton refuses added tasks and revisions, so the one window in which a fix task can be added is shorter than the 20 minute watch and the review-block fix can no longer be applied (`90d60001` hit this at 10:44 and 11:00; its four findings are listed on the Automaton). Fix: keep a review-blocked Automaton in `needs_review` until an operator or the supervisor acts, and let a blocked Automaton accept the fix task. |

## Internal bugs found during development (B-numbers)

Error-Filing Rule (AGENT.md): every internal bug found while developing or testing a feature gets a B-number row here, fixed in the branch that finds it; no GitHub issue.

| B# | Item | Status | Found by |
|---|---|---|---|
| B127 | The repo CI watch's `security_workflows` default became an empty list after a config save and a daemon restart: a nil list was written as `[]` and read back as "watch no security workflows", which silently switched the security failure pass off in every mode. Fixed: the default list is populated in `config.DefaultConfig`, so a save writes the names and a reload keeps them; an explicit empty list still means none. Tests: `internal/config/repo_ci_watch_security_workflows_test.go`. | **fixed** in branch `automaton/f1f20052` (rules-review pass) | sandbox live check, shadow then on |
| B128 | The comm verbs (`commGet` and `commJSON` in `internal/router/sx2_parity.go`) sent no `Authorization` header, so `autonomous security-failure` and every other comm verb answered HTTP 401 on a daemon with `server.token` set. Fixed: the router takes the token (`SetWebToken`, wired in `cmd/datawatch/main.go` for both the router and the `testRouter` used by `/api/test/message`) and presents it on every loopback call. Tests: `internal/router/security_failure_comm_auth_test.go`. | **fixed** in branch `automaton/f1f20052` (rules-review pass) | sandbox live check, shadow then on |
| B129 | The daemon's boot path did not carry the `repo_ci_watch` block into the autonomous Manager: after a restart the watch read `mode: off` until the block was saved again, so the security failure pass stayed off across a restart. Fixed: `repoCIWatchFromYAML` (`cmd/datawatch/live_autonomous_config.go`) is used in the boot bridge in `cmd/datawatch/main.go`; it keeps a nil security list (default) apart from an explicit empty one. Tests: `cmd/datawatch/repo_ci_watch_boot_test.go`. | **fixed** in branch `automaton/f1f20052` (rules-review pass) | sandbox live check, restart after shadow then on |
| B130 | Phone-width clipping in the PWA dialogs: the top of the first line of a question's answer dialog (and of any dialog opened through the shared `showModal`) was cut off and unreachable. Root cause: the question id line, the first child of the scrolling dialog body, carried a negative top margin (`margin:-8px 0 8px` in `internal/server/web/app.js`), which pushed the first line above the scroll origin. Fixed: the negative top margin is removed, `.generic-modal-body` gets a small top padding (4px) plus `overflow-anchor: none` (`internal/server/web/style.css`), and `showModal` resets the body's `scrollTop` to 0 on open and on its first `visualViewport` fit pass; the text still scrolls and the buttons stay in view. Tests: `internal/server/web/app-modal-scroll.test.js`, `internal/server/web/app-questions-clarity.test.js`. | **fixed** in branch `automaton/202a0003` | phone report, question answer dialog on a narrow viewport |

| 381 | Code-scanning alert 758 (rule go/uncontrolled-allocation-size, high) on `internal/autonomous/decision_record_store.go`: the decision record list sized its result slice from the caller limit, already clamped to 1000, but the scanner does not see the clamp through `min`. The list now starts from a small constant capacity and appends; a test lists with limits up to the largest integer and checks it returns at most the maximum without error. The other decision record code (folder watch, sync, server and MCP routes) was checked and sizes allocations only from data already in memory. | done, shipped 9.102.1 (patch); alert closes on the next CodeQL run after the push | 262 |

| 382 | Pattern found 2026-11-06 12:20 (affects the throughput of `61c60001`, `6aca0001`, and through them `90d60001`, `ec310001`): the sandbox live-check tasks written for the pi worker on the 27 billion parameter local model hang. Six runs so far (sessions `8be8`, `36f2`, `b004`, `1f52`, `64c5` and the first `ff17000b`) wrote a script early and then sat for one to three hours with no file written and near zero CPU; one attempt reported a 7120 second duration with an empty diff. Each hung run held a pi slot, which also queued the merge-back fix (`3b7c0001`) and the final gate of `c4880001` behind it. I ended the hung sessions by their IDs three times. Hypothesis: the worker starts the sandbox daemon from its shell tool, the tool call never returns, so pi waits for ever; a Claude worker handles this. Needed: a watchdog that ends a worker with no file or output activity for 30 minutes and counts it as an infra failure, a spec hint for live-check tasks (start the daemon detached with output redirected), and a rule that live-check tasks run on the Claude lane. Held by the burn guard at the moment (hold_claude), so the operator decides whether those two tasks may use Claude. Draft PRD to file. |
| 383 | Finding 2026-11-06 13:20: the verifier of the `61c60001` live-check task could not start for over 20 minutes because the johnnyjohnny GPU had 980 MB free against the 1,024 MB verifier minimum (verifier headroom 980 MB free is below the minimum). The 32 GB card holds the 27 billion parameter worker model (22.5 GB with its context), the daemon's default small model `qwen3:1.7b` (6.6 GB for a 1.7 billion parameter model because it loads with the same 64k context), an embedding model, and about 0.4 GB of desktop use. Unloaded the small model by hand (it reloads on demand and would take the memory again). Open for the operator: load that small model with a small context or on the datawatch node, since it is the daemon's default `ollama.model`; and the verifier minimum is a hard stop 44 MB short of satisfied. Not changed by the supervisor (configuration). |
| 384 | Shipped 2026-11-06 (9.103.1, patch): the Release workflow run for v9.103.0 failed at `containers / agent-base`, step Scan agent-base (Trivy, blocking), on `golang.org/x/net` v0.60.0, CVE-2026-78661 (HIGH, denial of service in `golang.org/x/net/html`, fixed in 0.62.0). The library sat in the `usr/bin/gh` binary, which the `ghbuilder` stage builds with `GH_XNET_VERSION`; that pin moved from 0.60.0 to 0.62.0 and datawatch's own go.mod moved to x/net v0.62.0 (Go also raised x/crypto, x/sys, x/term, x/text, x/sync, x/tools). A fix exists, so it was consumed rather than suppressed; nothing was added to security/accepted-risks.yml. No other go.mod in the repo and no vendored copy exist. | done, shipped with this row | 383 |

| 385 | Probable daemon defect found 2026-11-06 16:20 while debugging the archive grace live check: an Automaton whose only task completed stayed `running` because the completion roll-up aborted as stale (`rollup aborted (stale): completed -> completed`). The generated-artefact writer (`writeGeneratedArtefacts` in `internal/autonomous/generated_artefacts.go`) saves the Automaton with a plain `SavePRD`, so its revision bump is not counted as the roll-up's own save and `commitRollupStatus` aborts. With the seven generated artefact switches off in the sandbox the Automaton completed. This may also explain the Automata that sat at "stuck running: every task terminal and no live session or executor" and were then blocked (`90d60001`, `ec310001`). Draft PRD to file: count the artefact writer's own saves in the roll-up's staleness check, and add a test that completes an Automaton with all artefacts on. |
| 386 | Shipped 2026-11-06 (9.104.1, patch): CI run 42299831001 and the Release run 42299834424 for v9.104.0 failed in `go test ./internal/autonomous`, test `TestProtectedPathsAnswerRejectRealGitRestoresInNewCommit`: the reject answer ended blocked with the hold reason "the restore could not be committed: restore commit: git commit ... Author identity unknown", because the CI runner has no global git identity, so v9.104.0 was never published. Cause: `restoreTaskCommit` in `internal/autonomous/protected_paths_answer.go` ran `git commit` without an identity, while the task merge path already prefixes `gitIdentityArgs`. It passed on the development host only because that host has a global git identity. Fixed by running the restore commit through `gitIdentityArgs` (the daemon identity fallback applies only when the repository has no user.name and user.email). Reproduced first with an empty HOME and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, failing before and passing after; the other protected-paths git calls (checkout of the base version) need no identity. Released as v9.104.1; the v9.104.0 tag stays as it is and its content ships in 9.104.1. No workflow was rerun. |
| 387 | Gap found 2026-11-06 17:25 (operator: why do the guardrails and other sessions not have the auto tags, especially if they are tied to an Automaton, an Automation or a project): `TagTaskPlacement` has one caller, the task worker launch at `internal/autonomous/executor.go` (role worker). Every other session the Automata system starts is never tagged: the verifier sessions, the planner and decompose sessions, the rules review and guardrail judge sessions, the auto-fix and rejudge sessions, and the supervisor loop's own sessions, although the placement model already has the roles verifier, planner and judge and the kind decompose. Also the default axes are only compute, peer, backend, lane and role (project is off by default; the Automaton, Automation, stage and kind facts are reachable only through custom axes), so even a tagged worker carries no Automaton or project tag unless the Automaton itself is tagged. Needs: tag every session at the point the Automata system launches it, turn on Automaton and project axes by default (decision wanted from the operator on which axes), and the sweep that re-derives tags for existing sessions (asked earlier, not yet answered). Draft PRD to file once the operator picks the axes. |
| 388 | Defect found 2026-11-06 17:26 (operator: why does the 10.0.0 status only show 45 PRDs and not the full count from 9.0 to 10.0): v10-status counts no archived Automaton. `scripts/v10-status.py` and the progress engine (`datawatch autonomous progress`) both read `prd-list` and skip the status archived as an abandoned record, but since the supervisor loop closes a merged Automaton by archiving it, the finished work is exactly what is archived. The list holds 543 records created from 2026-09-11 to now: 488 archived, of which 331 have every task completed (2,338 tasks), plus 55 open. The status shows 0 done, "few Automata have finished" and 0.0 Automata a day, while the daily progress history shows 170 completed on 2026-10-24 rising to 315 on 2026-11-05 (about 12 a day). The completion estimates (11 to 260 days) are therefore wrong. Fix: count an archived record whose tasks are all completed as done in both the script and the Go progress engine (they share a golden test), keep other archived records excluded, and show the totals by project and arc once the Projects tab exists. Draft PRD to file with the project and arc work. |

| 410 | CORRECTED 2026-11-07 09:10: rows written earlier today (07:20 and 08:27) called pi workers "dead at launch" and the supervisor ended sessions 2594, a4f6, 0ed1, 66d8 and 094b by hand. That diagnosis was wrong or unproven: a pi worker runs in `--print` mode and writes its output log only at the end, and the `pi` process's command line does not contain the session id, so a grep for the session id shows only the `tee` and `cat` helpers. The reliable check is a process whose working directory is the task's worktree (here pid 3921140, `pi`, 39 minutes, with edited files) plus recent file changes. Several of those sessions may have been alive and slow, so their work was lost and the tasks re-ran; the ghost capacity leases after a kill are a separate, real defect (see row 411). The draft Automaton `28b30001` rested on the wrong premise and was rejected. What remains true and should be fixed: a hand kill must release the capacity lease; and a live pi worker is invisible between launch and finish, so the watch cannot tell slow from stuck, which needs a liveness line (process in the worktree, files changed, last change time). Lesson: never end a worker for a quiet log; check the process by working directory and the file times first. | corrected; replacement draft pending the hold |

| 428 | Drafts filed 2026-11-08 from the first real run of the new CodeQL gate and a long verifier: `cfd10001` the alert gate stops at once when the rate limit cannot reset inside its wait budget (the first real run waited 1500 seconds although the reset was about 50 minutes away, then failed with the clear not-a-code-problem message; the design held: it never reported a missing analysis); `5ab00001` a hard run cap for verifiers (setting `autonomous.verifier_max_run_seconds`, default 2700): one verifier ran more than two hours, alive and compacting its context, because the only limit is a no-progress timeout, and held a pi slot while five requests waited. Also learned: a verifier writes only to its pi session log (`~/.pi/agent/sessions/`), not to its worktree, so liveness is judged from that log, never from file changes in the worktree; background waits must not use `pgrep -f` on their own command text. Decomposed after the weekly budget reset. |

| 461 | Shipped 2026-11-10 in v9.126.0 (minor): Automaton `d2f10001`, merge shadow mode (Phase 4 of the autonomy handover plan). In place: when an Automaton finishes, the daemon plans its merge in the background — a trial merge of the work branch in a throwaway worktree, conflicts classified by file class, the build, lint and test gates run on the trial tree, the changelog entry read from the entry task, and the release size worked out against the base version — and stores the plan under the data directory with `applied` always `false`; it never changes the project branch or checkout and the finish path never waits on it. After the real merge lands, the shadow comparison reads the landed state from the base ref, grades each difference major or minor, and records each as a defect candidate (stored, content-keyed, not filed into the defect queue). Checks in the plan: unmerged task branch, stray root file, review head, lint version pin, load-sensitive test gate, failing test names, gate process group kill. Settings `autonomous.merge_shadow.enabled` (default on), `.gate_timeout` (default `20m`) and `.plan_keep` (default 200) are validated and set on REST, MCP, CLI and chat; the state is read on `GET /api/autonomous/merge/shadow`, the `autonomous_merge_shadow_status` MCP tool, `datawatch autonomous merge-shadow status` and `compare`, the chat verb `autonomous merge-shadow`, and a Merge shadow card in the PWA Setup and policy tab (all read-only). | 469 | Shipped 2026-11-10 in v9.133.0 (minor): Automaton `5ab00001`, the verifier run cap. In place: every verifier session has a hard maximum run time, `autonomous.verifier_max_run_seconds` (whole seconds, default 2700, `0` = no cap, negative or non-whole refused on every surface), counted from the capacity grant and enforced in the verifier watchdog even while the verifier keeps producing output; a session that reaches the cap is ended, its worker slot is freed, the decision log gets a `verifier_run_cap` entry, the task attempt ends with reason `verifier_max_run`, and the verification is retried once on the next backend in the verifier policy order (a single-backend policy gets no retry; the retry runs on that backend's registry default model, or the pi-family guard). A cut run counts as verifier infrastructure trouble, not a content failure. `GET /api/capacity` `verifier` block gains `run_cap_seconds` (cap in effect), `run_cap_ends` and a `run_cap` category (the spec asked for the cap under `run_max_seconds`; that field already means the longest observed run, so the cap is `run_cap_seconds`). The key is settable from PWA Settings (General), REST config, CLI `config set`, MCP `autonomous_config_set` (`verifier_max_run_seconds`), chat `configure` and YAML. Two new locale keys in en, de, es, fr and ja. PWA changed (`internal/server/web/app.js`, the five locale files, `internal/server/web/verifier_run_cap_settings.test.js`). Audit action `autonomous.verifier_run_cap` now has its own CEF signature (86), added at merge. Not validated against a live daemon: no cap-ended session was run before release. The 2700-second default was not chosen by the operator. The howto `docs/howto/automaton-verifier-run-cap.md` still carries two open "for the operator to confirm" notes (retry model wording, default against real durations). The branch also carried gofmt-only churn in `internal/server/api.go`.
